## Re-derivation 2026-07-25 (18:20) — stub -> real brownfield contract

- (decision) OQ-1 RESOLVED (operator, 2026-07-25): Atlas WILL eventually expose a public, versioned API tier beyond agent-mediated access — deferred as a real capability, not closed as a non-goal. - (decision) OQ-2 RESOLVED (from evidence, no operator decision needed): an OpenSSF-Scorecard-class maintenance signal is a WARDEN AXIS, not an Atlas feed — Atlas measures, Warden judges. pyforge-warden's Dream already names six axes (hygiene, security, license, currency, provenance, maintenance), with maintenance as axis 5-6 at Vision tier; v1 gates on the first four. This is the standing architectural split and the same separation the Charter states as 'the hand that builds is never the gate that judges'. Atlas supplies the signal; Warden owns the verdict.

- (change) AUD-ATLAS-010/013 (Epic 10 / I0): declared 15 conda run-deps + 15 wheel deps derived by AST scan of module-level imports (3 declared vs 19 imported) in pixi.toml + pyproject.toml; kedro-test went 17 collection errors -> 781 passed. Moved the misfiled test_sentinel_knowledge.py out of the package to src/sentinel/tests/ (it imports sentinel.knowledge.*, a different project).
- (change) AUD-ATLAS-046 (Epic 10 / I1): orchestration/definitions.py module docstring retracted its false run-admission claim. The in_process executor serializes ops WITHIN a run and gives no cross-run/cross-process admission; the comment asserted otherwise. Tracked DW-AD23-1; AD-23 demoted in the spine until it ships with a gate.
- (decision) Recorded residual AUD-ATLAS-050: semantic/models.py hard-imports boring_semantic_layer, which is PyPI-only (no conda-forge candidate) and therefore cannot be a conda run-dep — a pure-conda install cannot import that module. Supplied to the env instead; the real fix is a lazy/guarded import, deferred to its own story.
- (change) Epic 10 prerequisite — the offline orchestration gate was never hermetic. `orchestration/definitions.py` builds its Dagster `Definitions` at MODULE IMPORT (Dagster's code-location convention, not fixable by laziness), which resolves the full catalog; two catalog entries declare `credentials:` keys, and Kedro raises KeyError when one is absent — so the failure landed at pytest COLLECTION, before any fixture could intervene. Those credentials live only in the gitignored conf/local/credentials.yml, so `kedro-test` was green on the authoring machine and red at collection in every fresh clone, CI runner, and bmad-loop story worktree (0 of 787 collectable). Found when the loop's own [verify] gate could not pass inside a worktree the loop had just created. Added tests/orchestration/conftest.py: seeds a STUB conf/local/credentials.yml iff none exists (an existing file is never read, overwritten, or logged), with the required key NAMES derived from catalog.yml so a newly credentialed dataset cannot silently re-break collection.
- (decision) Stub credentials kept OUT of conf/base, deliberately, against two cheaper options. (a) Shipping conf/base/credentials.yml would also hand stubs to LIVE runs, converting a clear config-time failure into a confusing 401 at the HTTP layer — a real run SHOULD fail loudly on missing credentials; it also trips tests/catalog/test_credential_scoping.py, a bright line left absolute rather than relaxed. (b) Adding conf/local/credentials.yml to bmad-loop's `worktree_seed` would have fixed the loop only, not fresh clones or CI, and would have copied REAL credential material into every story worktree. The chosen fix follows the pattern tests/catalog/conftest.py already set (STUB_CREDENTIALS injected by the harness), which is why kedro-catalog-check was already hermetic.
- (event) Verified in a clean room (the loop home, which has no credentials file at all): 0 collectable -> 781 passed / 6 failed / 19 skipped, byte-identical to the main checkout, whose real credentials file was confirmed unmodified by sha256 before/after. kedro-catalog-check still 47 passed — the credential-scoping guard untouched. The residual 6 failures are AUD-ATLAS-011 (I3), unrelated to this fix.
- (change) Epic 10 written into planning-artifacts/epics.md (6 stories, I0-I5) — it existed only in sprint-change-proposal-2026-07-27.md and sprint-status.yaml, so the loop's story-drafting step had no source to draft from. Recorded outside the 1->9 wave chain (it repairs what the waves left divergent) and ordered by the VERIFY GATE rather than by wave: I3 first, because kedro-test is red on main until it lands and every later story would inherit a failing gate. Also noted: the proposal's own I1-I4 enumeration is superseded by sprint-status.yaml's i0-i5, which is what bmad-loop actually reads.
- (decision) I5 / AUD-ATLAS-046 MECHANISM CLOSED by the operator 2026-07-28, BEFORE the story was drafted, and encoded as binding ACs D1-D6 in epics.md Story I5 (+ the sprint feed's q_gate). The loop implements them; it does not re-decide. Grounding verified in code first: writes are Parquet under `data/` (env-overridable), and EVERY DuckDB connection in the package is argless — `duckdb.connect()` / `ibis.duckdb.connect()` — i.e. in-memory. DuckDB is the query engine over Parquet, not a persistent store. The concurrency surface is therefore multiple PROCESSES on ONE machine sharing a POSIX filesystem: the 7 MCP `run_*` tools (each calling KedroSession.run), the CLI, and — once DW-C1-1 lands — Dagster.
- (decision) D1 mechanism = OS file lock (`filelock`, ALREADY in the pyforge-atlas env, so no new dependency). DB lock REJECTED: there is no database to lock, so it would mean CREATING a persistent store purely for coordination — a new failure domain — and DuckDB permits one writer process anyway, i.e. its own file lock with extra steps. Dagster run-queue (QueuedRunCoordinator + tag_concurrency_limits) REJECTED as the v1 answer: it governs only runs passing through the Dagster daemon, and the MCP tools never touch Dagster — so it would guard the one entry point that is NOT the risk, leave the agent-facing path unguarded, and cannot even be demonstrated today (daemon deferred, DW-C1-1). Shipping it would satisfy the story's letter while leaving AD-23's actual property false — precisely the defect class AUD-ATLAS-046 raised.
- (decision) D2 placement = a Kedro hook in `settings.HOOKS` (acquire in before_pipeline_run; release in BOTH after_pipeline_run AND on_pipeline_error). This is the load-bearing half: HOOKS is already the seam validation (F2) and observability (E2) use for exactly this reason — every entry point reaches KedroSession.run, which is what AD-23's "identical machinery" clause rests on. Admission logic is BANNED from mcp/, from orchestration/definitions.py, and from node bodies.
- (decision) D3 reject-not-queue (bounded wait opt-in only), with a typed error naming the locked dataset(s), holding run id and hold start — because the caller is usually an agent over MCP, where a silent block is indistinguishable from a hang. D4 granularity = the pipeline's declared OUTPUT dataset set, one lock per dataset, acquired in SORTED name order (deadlock avoidance, free); a single global lock is rejected as both over-serializing (seed_gaps vs vulnerability) and an overclaim, since AD-23 says per target dataset set. D5 stale locks record holder PID + start time and are reclaimable when the PID is dead — a SIGKILL'd run must never wedge the factory permanently.
- (decision) D6: the "record the absence as a contract-level non-goal" escape hatch is WITHDRAWN. AD-23 currently sits DEMOTED in ARCHITECTURE-SPINE because the claim outran the code; documenting the absence would leave a spine invariant permanently half-stated, and the build is small. On green the story RE-PROMOTES AD-23 to its full form and corrects the retracted docstring in orchestration/definitions.py. Stated boundary, written down rather than discovered later: file locks do not hold across machines (NFS flock unreliable) — atlas is single-machine today, and a multi-machine atlas re-opens D1. Dagster's run-queue is complementary later, never the safety property, because it cannot see MCP or CLI.
- (constraint) I5's verify gate REQUIRES a real TWO-PROCESS test — a second OS process, not a thread and not a mock. A single-process test does not discharge the AC, because the defect being fixed is cross-process; a threaded test would pass against a lock that provides no cross-process exclusion at all.
- (event) I4 / AUD-ATLAS-043 CRITICAL escalation 2026-07-28, and the loop was RIGHT. The dev session implemented the original ACs faithfully (all 6 tasks, 790/790 tests green, kedro-catalog-check green), then its own review pass found the defect was in the INTENT CONTRACT rather than the code, reverted the whole implementation, and escalated instead of shipping. The original AC specified `read_dataset`'s `build_stamp` as wall-clock-NOW at call time — which can never distinguish fresh data from stale (every read of a month-old dataset reports "now"), contradicting AD-13 ("republication never launders freshness"), SPEC.md's own AD-17 definition (a payload carries THE PIPELINE'S build timestamp, not a read receipt), and the Wave-H CompileCrew precedent of forwarding SOURCE staleness rather than fabricating a new timestamp at republish time. This is the same defect class as AUD-ATLAS-046 — a claim the implementation cannot back — caught before shipping this time.
- (decision) BOTH options the escalation offered are REJECTED, and its blocking premise was FALSE. It asked for either (a) "find or build a genuine per-dataset freshness signal — investigation found none exists anywhere in the catalog today" or (b) "rename the field away from build_stamp/AD-17 framing to an honest response-generation receipt". (b) deletes the requirement instead of meeting it — it keeps a useless value and relabels it. (a) is unnecessary because the premise is wrong: `IncrementalParquetDataset` ALREADY "stamps + round-trips a per-row `fetched_at` epoch timestamp and owns the TTL freshness verdict", and 15 catalog entries use it. The signal exists, is already persisted, and needs no inventing. Verified by reading the dataset class rather than taking the escalation's conclusion at face value.
- (decision) Option (c) adopted by the operator and encoded as BINDING ACs C1-C6 in epics.md Story I4. C1: stamp from the DATA's own provenance per kind, exhaustively over the 75 catalog entries — IncrementalParquetDataset (15) uses its own `fetched_at`; pandas.ParquetDataset (22) uses the materialized file mtime; api.APIDataset (24) legitimately uses `now`, because for a live read the call IS the fetch; everything else is `null` + a stated reason. C2: the envelope names WHICH kind it got (`provenance_kind`), because "the data is from then" and "we called the API just now" are opposite meanings and conflating them is the whole of AUD-ATLAS-043. C3: row-level provenance carries the oldest+newest range and judges staleness on the OLDEST (a single summary timestamp on a partially refreshed dataset is itself a small laundering). C4: `null` is a REQUIRED valid answer — fabricating a plausible value is the defect being removed. C5: `schema_version` on the envelope (folds in the escalation's one `bad_spec` finding, which stands regardless of approach). C6: closes the AUD-ATLAS-044 half — every Vizro page carries its data's stamp, not just `factory-status`.
- (constraint) I4's verify gate now requires PER-KIND envelope tests proving a PERSISTED dataset reports its own recorded provenance and NOT the read time. A test that merely asserts "a stamp is present" does not discharge C1 — the rejected implementation passed exactly that test. The reverted patch is deliberately NOT restored (`resolve --no-interactive`, not `--restore-patch`): it faithfully implemented the wrong approach, so re-driving from the corrected contract is the point.
- (event) Owed I5 follow-up review discharged 2026-07-30 (review pass 5 on `spec-10-6`). Pass 4 had left `followup_review_recommended: true` because its 7 medium patches to `admission.py`'s acquire/release paths had never been seen by an independent reviewer, and three prior reading-passes had still left two vacuous tests behind. Pass 5 therefore used a different lens — adversarial mutation rather than re-reading: 8 mutants injected one at a time over the pass-4 surfaces (`_write_holder` atomicity, `release()`'s unlink/release order, its failed-release repair, its malformed-ticket padding, `_release_one`'s never-raises contract, and all three `default_lock_root` decisions), with the 98-test suite as oracle. 7 caught, **1 survived**: `release()`'s `names += [None] * (len(locks) - len(names))` padding could be deleted with every test still green, so the tail-lock leak it prevents — locks held for the life of the process, the unreclaimable state correctness requirement 1 exists to prevent — was unverified, as was the `logger.error` its own comment promises. Closed by `test_release_frees_the_tail_when_a_ticket_has_fewer_names_than_locks`; re-injecting the mutant now fails it, so the mutation score is 8/8. `kedro-test` 901 → **902 passed / 19 skipped**. Zero behavioural defects found, which is the falling find-rate pass 4 asked to see before converging.
- (assumption) `DW-AD23-3` independently corroborated and deliberately left open. Reading `default_lock_root` for the mutation work confirmed the deferral is real and correctly characterized: the env chain ends in the literal default `"data"`, so with neither `PYFORGE_ATLAS_LOCK_ROOT` nor `PYFORGE_ATLAS_DATA_ROOT` set the lock store is `<project_root>/data/.locks` — inside the tree the locks guard, so `rm -rf data/` can unlink a live holder's inode. Worth separating: the module's *anchoring* argument (project root, never CWD) is sound and well covered — mutating the env precedence, the whitespace-strip strictness, and the absolute-override branch were each caught. It is the default VALUE that is hazardous, not the anchoring.
- (event) Story 10.5's spec duplication resolved. The project carried two 10-5 specs — a 174-line drafted contract at `status: ready-for-dev` and a 706-line completed record at `status: 'done'` with `final_revision` — the only re-drive spec pair in the fleet. The draft was the sole non-terminal spec in the project, which read as an unfinished story when 10.5 had in fact landed in PR #132. Marked `superseded` (a new value in this repo's spec vocabulary) with a `superseded_by:` pointer and a banner, rather than `done`, so one story does not appear to have two completed specs; kept rather than deleted because it is the original drafted contract and this project has lost story specs to Tier-3 teardown before.
- (event) Owed 10.5 follow-up review discharged 2026-07-30 (review pass 5 on `spec-10-5-…-2`), same adversarial-mutation lens as 10.6's. Pass 4's flag named its exposure exactly — a "NEW speculative-parse fallback branch plus 2 new degrade paths in `resolve_for_file` that no independent reviewer has seen" — so 7 mutants were injected over those surfaces with the 902-test gate as oracle. 5 caught. Two survived, and they are NOT the same kind of thing, which is the useful part: **N3** (dropping the `not is_numeric_dtype` guard on the speculative fallback) survived **correctly** — for a numeric column `_to_epoch_seconds` only yields `NaN` where the input was `NaN`, so `seconds.empty` implies all-NULL and `pd.to_datetime` then yields all-`NaT`, making the branch unreachable by construction; verified empirically across raw epoch-ns ints, `YYYYMMDD`-looking ints and all-NULL floats, guard ON and OFF identical. The guard is belt-and-braces and the "never re-interpreted as a date" property comes from the ORDERING, not the guard — recorded so nobody later closes it with a test that cannot fail. **N7** was a real vacuity: collapsing `resolve_for_file`'s non-ENOENT `OSError` branch into the `"not found"` reason left all 902 tests green while the function reported a file that EXISTS as absent — the same false-reason class pass 4 had just fixed one function over in `_resolve_row_fetched_at`, on one of the two `resolve_for_file` paths the flag named, and its sibling directory case was tested while this one was not. Fixed by `test_resolve_for_file_says_unreadable_not_missing` (reproduced live via a `chmod 000` parent; skipped under euid 0). `kedro-test` 902 → 903 passed, `kedro-catalog-check` 47/47.
- (assumption) Both Epic 10 follow-up reviews are now discharged and no atlas Epic-10 spec asks for another. The three that remain (`spec-b1`, `spec-b2`, `spec-b5`) are Wave-B migration stories from a different era and were NOT reviewed here; the same mutation lens applies to them and they are the honest remainder of "atlas is closed out". Recording the pattern that made both Epic-10 passes productive: mutate only the surfaces the previous pass CHANGED, use the project's own gate as the oracle rather than a hand-picked subset, and treat a surviving mutant as a hypothesis to be proved harmful before calling it a defect — one of the four survivors across the two stories turned out to be a genuine no-op, and adding a test for it would have been vacuous.
- (event) `DW-AD23-3` FIXED 2026-07-30, so the `(assumption)` two entries up — "independently corroborated and deliberately left open" — no longer holds; it is left as written because it is the pass-5 record. The default lock store moved from the data tree's CHILD `<data_root>/.locks` to its SIBLING `<data_root>.locks`, so `rm -rf data/` can no longer delete a lock file out from under a live holder. Pass 5's separation was exactly right and is what made the fix small: the *anchoring* argument needed no change at all — project-root-not-CWD, read-env-before-resolving-the-base (so the absolute-override escape hatch stays reachable in an installed layout), the whitespace-strip strictness — all preserved and still gated by their existing tests. Only the default VALUE moved. SPEC.md's fourth AD-23 boundary is restated to match.
- (decision) The store stays DERIVED FROM the data root rather than pinned to the project. `<project>/.locks` was the obvious alternative and is wrong: two checkouts pointed at one shared `PYFORGE_ATLAS_DATA_ROOT` write the same Parquet and must contend, and a project-pinned store would have given them one store each — the same silent voiding of admission that CWD-anchoring caused, reached through a different door. Pinned by `test_one_shared_data_root_yields_one_store_across_two_project_roots`.
- (decision) BOTH branches of the ledger's resolution were taken, not just the first. `PYFORGE_ATLAS_LOCK_ROOT` resolving inside the data root is now REFUSED (`AdmissionConfigError`, before any lock is taken) — fixing the default while leaving the override able to undo it silently would fix nothing, the same reasoning that made a relative `lock_root=` a refusal rather than a warning. The check is advisory-if-unresolvable by necessity: an installed layout with no `conf/base/catalog.yml` is precisely the case an absolute `PYFORGE_ATLAS_LOCK_ROOT` exists to serve, so an unresolvable data root must not turn that hatch back off (`_data_root_if_resolvable`).
- (constraint) What the fix does NOT close, pinned as a test rather than left in prose: unlinking a lock file still admits a second writer, because `flock` belongs to the inode. No placement can prevent that; the fix only removes the ROUTINE way to reach it. `test_unlinking_the_lock_file_itself_still_admits_a_second_writer` asserts the limitation, so a future change claiming to have fixed it has to red that test first. The ledger resolution's literal wording ("assert a second acquirer is still refused" after unlinking a held lock) was NOT implementable as written — it asks for a kernel property `flock` does not have — so it was aimed at the operator action it actually meant: `test_clearing_the_data_tree_leaves_a_held_lock_still_excluding` rmtree's the data root mid-hold and asserts the rejection.
- (event) Member `.gitignore` gains `data.locks/` — the `data/**` rule no longer covers the store, which is the one real cost of moving it out of the data tree. Gates: `kedro-test` 903 → **911 passed / 19 skipped** (+8, exactly the new cases: 5 test functions, one parametrized three ways); `kedro-catalog-check` 47; `dagster-dryrun` 58; `deferred-work-check` green.

## Surface reconcile 2026-08-09 — steward story 7-1 landing (one build, whole Guild)

- (event) Two governed paths moved under this Spec's surface when `pyforge-steward`'s story `7-1-one-build-whole-guild` (`spec-unified-container` CAP-1) landed a cross-project fix, not an atlas-authored story: CHANGED `src/shared/packages/pyforge-atlas/src/pyforge/atlas/__main__.py` — intercepts a root-only `--version` (scoped to `cli_args[0] == "--version"`, so `pyforge-atlas run --version` still reaches Kedro's own usage path) before `configure_project`/`find_run_command`, printing `pyforge-atlas {__version__}` and returning before Kedro dispatch — closes the one CLI-contract gap named in the container epic's AC (atlas was one of two stations with no `--version`). ADDED `src/shared/packages/pyforge-atlas/tests/test_main_version.py` (3 tests, incl. the position-scoping fix) — regression coverage for the new flag. No capability, constraint, or AD changed; this is a CLI-contract completion, not new atlas scope. Verified on the landing branch: `pyforge-atlas --version` exits 0; `kedro-test` green. Baseline scoped-stamped to this spec only.
- (change) RECONCILED BY NAME, 2026-08-09 — steward story `7-1`'s follow-up review pass, same two paths, no new ones. CHANGED `.../atlas/__main__.py`: the Kedro imports (`find_run_command`, `configure_project`) moved from module scope to INSIDE `main()`, below the `--version` intercept. Importing `kedro.framework.project` installs Kedro's rich logging config at import time and prints an INFO banner to STDOUT, so the shipped `pyforge-atlas --version` was emitting NINE lines — eight of them a wrapped absolute path from the build host's checkout — instead of the one clean line every other station prints. Verified in the real image: 9 lines -> 1, rc 0, no path leak. Same commit also honors Click's `main(args=[...])` keyword form in the intercept (the dispatch below it already forwarded `**kwargs`, so keyword callers silently bypassed `--version`). CHANGED `.../tests/test_main_version.py`: 3 tests -> 5. The three original `capsys` tests structurally CANNOT catch the noise regression (Kedro's banner fires at collection, outside capsys's window, so they stayed green while the CLI was broken); added a subprocess test asserting exactly one stdout line, a keyword-form test, and an exit-code assertion on the position-scoping test (bare `pytest.raises(SystemExit)` passed for any exit, incl. unrelated failures — now pinned to Click's 2). Full atlas suite 916 passed; the 1 failure (`test_dashboard_dryrun.py::test_factory_status_reads_the_real_sprint_status`) reproduces with these changes stashed and is pre-existing. Contract unchanged. Baseline scoped-stamped to this spec only.
- (change) RECONCILED BY NAME, 2026-08-09 — steward story `7-1`'s THIRD review pass, same two paths, no new ones. CHANGED `src/shared/packages/pyforge-atlas/src/pyforge/atlas/__main__.py`: the positional branch of the `--version` arg resolution tested truthiness (`if args:`) while the keyword branch tested `is not None`, so the two disagreed about the same value — `main(None)`, Click's documented "read sys.argv" shape, produced a non-empty `(None,)` tuple, resolved `cli_args` to `None`, and silently skipped the intercept, while `main(args=None)` honored it. Both branches now test `is not None`. CHANGED `.../tests/test_main_version.py`: 5 tests -> 6. Added `test_explicit_none_positional_falls_back_to_sys_argv` for the shape above, and widened the subprocess test to assert on BOTH streams — it previously asserted only stdout, so a logging config emitted on stderr instead (the normal destination for a logging handler) would have kept it green while `pyforge-atlas --version 2>&1` stayed exactly as polluted as before the deferred-import fix; it now also asserts the checkout path is absent from the combined output. Verified: 6 passed; full atlas suite 917 passed / 1 failed, the failure being `tests/dashboard/test_dashboard_dryrun.py::test_factory_status_reads_the_real_sprint_status` — the same pre-existing one pass 2 recorded, in dashboard/sprint-ledger code these changes do not touch. Re-verified in the real image: `pyforge-atlas --version` -> one line, rc 0. Contract unchanged. Baseline scoped-stamped to this spec only.

## Surface reconcile 2026-08-09 — story 13-1 landing (GitHub-trending discovery, CAP-1/FR-64)

- (event) 21 governed paths moved under this Spec's surface when story `13-1-trending-ingest`
  (`spec-upstream-discovery` CAP-1) landed; naming each rather than stamping blind. New 8th
  pipeline: ADDED `src/pyforge/atlas/pipelines/upstream_discovery/{__init__,pipeline,nodes}.py`

  `tests/pipelines/upstream_discovery/test_nodes.py` — +7 cases pinning each patch

## Surface reconcile 2026-08-09 — story 13-3 follow-up review pass (10 patches)

- (change) FOLLOW-UP REVIEW PASS on the landed 13-3 surface; four governed paths CHANGED,
  one ADDED, no capability/constraint/AD change. The three landing-blockers, each verified
  live before and after:
  (1) `query.py` — the NaN→`None` step was a SILENT NO-OP on float columns.
  `DataFrame.where(cond, None)` does not upcast `float64`, so pandas cast the `None`
  straight back to `NaN`; only an all-null OBJECT column ever worked — which is precisely
  the single-row shape the guard test used, making it a FALSE GREEN. Any real frame with
  both a value and a null in one optional column (`stars_today`, null whenever a row lacks
  a daily delta) leaked the bare non-RFC-8259 `NaN` token into `--json`, breaking CAP-3's
  own literal success signal. Fixed with `.astype(object)` before the `where`; the test now
  uses two rows, pins the `float64` dtype it exists to cover, and re-parses with
  `parse_constant` rejecting.
  (2) `__main__.py` — `--json` stdout was not pipeable. Kedro's rich handler sits on the
  ROOT logger writing to STDOUT and the root level is WARNING, so the INFO-only
  `logging.disable` floor let every WARNING record (e.g. kedro's own "Credentials not
  found…") print INTO the envelope. Floor raised to `logging.CRITICAL` for the JSON path
  only; the test that "took the last non-empty line" — a workaround that tolerated exactly
  this defect — now parses the WHOLE of stdout.
  (3) `query.py` — the period/tier/reason filters were guarded by `and "<col>" in
  df.columns`, so a frame missing a column silently SKIPPED that filter and returned every
  row while `filters` still reported the value the caller asked for. Verified live: no
  `reason` column returned an already-on-conda-forge repo under the default `--not-on-cf`
  — the one row CAP-5's Mason handoff must never be given. Each now empties instead,
  mirroring the `stars_total` branch and the Boundaries & Constraints rule it already
  stated. A filter not requested (`period="all"`, `tier="all"`, `not_on_cf=False`) needs no
  column and is still skipped.
  Also: CLI error handling widened to cover the deferred `from . import query` and
  `json.dumps` (both previously escaped as raw tracebacks past a `finally`-only outer try),
  with the exception TYPE prefixed (a bootstrap `KeyError` had printed the bare line
  `'atlas'`); `logging.disable` now RESTORES the caller's prior floor instead of resetting
  to NOTSET (verified: a host at `disable(WARNING)` had its own suppression silently
  lifted); `_validate_period` strips like `_validate_tier` already did (same padding was
  accepted on one flag, rejected on the other); integral star counts stay `int` via the
  nullable `Int64` dtype (one unrelated null had flipped every OTHER row's emitted
  `stars_total` from `1200` to `1200.0`); table mode gained a provenance/staleness header
  — "never ingested" and "your filters matched nothing" were byte-identical `(no
  candidates)` output, and the staleness `catalog.yml` promises this surface always shows
  was visible only under `--json`, i.e. false for the DEFAULT human path the claim
  describes.
- (change) CHANGED `src/pyforge/atlas/mcp/audit.py` — ADDED `TRENDING_SURFACE_TOOLS =
  ("query_trending_candidates",)`. The tool shipped registered on the server but recorded
  in NO bucket of the module that is this package's declared, tested record of its MCP
  surface — the same omission Story 13.1 avoided (`run_upstream_discovery_pipeline` →
  `PIPELINE_TRIGGER_TOOLS`) and `query_vizro_ai` avoided (`NL_INTERFACE_TOOLS`). Nothing
  detected it, so a test now pins it and asserts every recorded tool is really registered.
- (change) ADDED `tests/trending_candidates/test_surface_parity.py`. The story's central
  claim is CLI/MCP identity BY CONSTRUCTION, but the filter DEFAULTS are declared FOUR
  times (`query.py`, `mcp/tools.py`, `mcp/server.py`, argparse) and each forwards its own
  literals, so `query.py`'s defaults are dead code on every other path. The concrete gap:
  change a default in `query.py` alone and the existing parity test still passes (both CLI
  sides move together) while the MCP tool keeps sending the old value — a DEFAULT
  invocation of the two surfaces returning different results, exactly what CAP-3's success
  signal forbids. All four declarations are now pinned to one source of truth
  (`server.py` read via AST, mirroring `test_no_business_logic_in_tool_bodies.py`).
- (event) Re-verified live: full `kedro-test` 1027 passed / 19 skipped; the isolated
  `pytest tests/trending_candidates tests/mcp -q` subset 74 passed (60 before this pass);
  `trending-candidates -- --json` piped through a strict `parse_constant` parser round-trips
  clean. Baseline scoped-stamped to this spec only.

## Surface reconcile 2026-08-09 — story 13-3 second follow-up review pass (11 patches)

- (change) SECOND FOLLOW-UP REVIEW on the landed 13-3 surface; five governed paths
  CHANGED, no capability/constraint/AD change. Every defect below was reproduced live
  before the fix and re-verified after. The behavioural ones:
  (1) `__main__.py` — the two print paths sat OUTSIDE the `except Exception` guard, in an
  outer `try` carrying only a `finally`, so a write failure escaped as a raw traceback.
  The ordinary `trending-candidates -- --json | head` produced exactly that
  (`BrokenPipeError`, verified live) — the one class of failure a CLI is most likely to
  meet, and precisely what the module documents can never happen. Both moved inside the
  guard, with a dedicated `BrokenPipeError` arm that detaches stdout onto `/dev/null`
  first so the interpreter's shutdown flush cannot print "Exception ignored in:
  <_io.TextIOWrapper …>" after a clean exit 1.
  (2) `query.py` — a non-finite `stars_total` aborted the WHOLE query. A corrupt cell
  coercing to ±inf (the literal `"inf"`, or an overflowing `"1e400"` — the same dirty
  STRING class the numeric coercion exists for) reached the integral narrowing and raised
  `OverflowError: cannot convert float infinity to integer`, where the contract promises
  ONE excluded row. Folded into NA so it takes the identical never-satisfies-the-floor
  path a null already takes; the narrowing additionally skips out-of-int64-range values.
  (3) `query.py` — the previous pass's NaN→`None` guard used `pd.notna`, which says True
  for ±inf, so `json.dumps` emitted the bare `Infinity` token: as non-RFC-8259 as the
  `NaN` that pass fixed, and rejected by any strict parser (verified live). Masked now.
  (4) `query.py` — the previous pass's integral-narrowing fix was applied to `stars_total`
  ALONE, but `stars_today` is null for 2 of the 3 fetched periods BY CONSTRUCTION and
  `forks_total` is null whenever the scrape lacks the tag, so those columns widened to
  `float64` and emitted `7.0` for the rows that DO carry a count — the identical "a count's
  JSON type depends on unrelated rows" defect, one column over. Generalized into
  `_narrow_integral_floats`, applied to every float column.
  (5) `query.py` — `--period all` made the documented "deterministic tie-break for --top's
  cap" false: the same repo contributes up to 3 rows tied on BOTH sort keys, so which
  survived `head(top)` was decided by the parquet file's physical row order (identical data
  re-materialized in a different order returned different rows, verified live). `period` is
  now the third sort key, which makes the ordering total.
- (change) CHANGED `src/pyforge/atlas/mcp/audit.py` — ADDED `GENERIC_SURFACE_TOOLS` +
  `registered_surface_tools()`. The previous pass's new audit test asserted only
  `recorded ⊆ registered`, which the very defect it was written for satisfies trivially: a
  tool registered on the server and recorded in NO bucket. The converse assertion is the
  one that fails for it, and it needs the module to state the WHOLE surface — the three
  generic read/list primitives were the only registered tools no bucket named.
- (change) CHANGED `conf/base/catalog.yml` — the `trending_candidates` block still read
  "CAP-3 (operator surface) through CAP-5 … pending" while the header this same story
  edited says CAP-3 landed. The story retired one of the two CAP-3-pending banners; the
  file contradicted itself about whether the operator surface exists.
- (change) CHANGED `__main__.py` comments/labels — the `--json` block claimed diagnostics
  "still reach the operator, on stderr, via the handler below" and there is NO such
  handler (`logging.disable(CRITICAL)` suppresses, including ERROR; re-pointing is not
  available from there, since kedro installs its stdout handler via its own `dictConfig`
  DURING the bootstrap). Corrected to say what it does, and to name where a `--json`
  operator DOES still see a failure: in-band in the envelope (`provenance_kind`/`reason`)
  and on stderr from the guard. The table header's `matched=` was renamed `shown=` — it
  carries the POST-cap count, so "matched" was simply false whenever `--top` truncated.
  A multi-line degradation `reason` now prefixes every line with `#` (an un-prefixed
  `[Errno 2] …` line sat between header and rows in the ordinary fresh-worktree run).
- (event) Re-verified live: full `kedro-test` 1035 passed / 19 skipped; the isolated
  `pytest tests/trending_candidates tests/mcp -q` subset 82 passed (74 before this pass).
  Both new guards MUTATION-tested: transposing two kwargs in `server.py`'s wrapper, and
  dropping one name from `audit.py`, each now fail — before this pass the whole suite
  stayed green through the kwarg transposition. `trending-candidates -- --json` still
  round-trips through a strict parser (`count: 0` — no parquet in a fresh worktree, so it
  exercises zero rows; the row-shaped defects above were all reproduced against seeded
  frames, not that run). Baseline scoped-stamped to this spec only.

## Surface reconcile 2026-08-10 — story 13-3 third follow-up review pass (7 patches)

- (change) THIRD FOLLOW-UP REVIEW on the landed 13-3 surface; five governed paths CHANGED,
  no capability/constraint/AD change. Every defect below was reproduced live before the fix
  and re-verified after — this time against a SEEDED 31-row parquet, not the empty
  fresh-worktree table the previous passes' live runs exercised (that gap is exactly why
  row-shaped defects kept surviving to landing; see the last pass's own closing note).
  (1) `__main__.py` — the previous pass's `BrokenPipeError` fix was a NO-OP for the case it
  was written for. stdout is BLOCK-buffered whenever it is a pipe and no realistic envelope
  fills that buffer, so `print` returned cleanly and the error surfaced only in the
  interpreter's SHUTDOWN flush, OUTSIDE the guard, as "Exception ignored while flushing
  sys.stdout" + exit 120. The pass verified its fix in a shell exporting `PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1`
  — ambient here, set by neither `pixi.toml` nor the Containerfile — which is precisely what
  hid it: with `env -u PYTHONUNBUFFERED … --json | head`, the guard never ran. An explicit
  `sys.stdout.flush()` inside the guard fixes it (now: exit 1, empty stderr).
  (2) `__main__.py` — that guard's `os.dup2` retargeted the descriptor for the WHOLE process
  and nothing ever undid it, so an in-process `main()` caller — the same callable API the
  previous pass added `test_json_restores_the_callers_own_logging_floor` for — came back with
  its own stdout permanently pointed at `/dev/null`, and the devnull fd leaked. Now guarded on
  `sys.stdout is sys.__stdout__` (a host that redirected stdout has no shutdown flush to
  silence anyway) and the fd is closed.
  (3) `query.py` + `__main__.py` — the envelope carried ONE count. `datasets/upstream_discovery.py`
  falls back to the Search API when the HTML scrape yields zero rows across all THREE windows
  and stamps every fallback row `period="all"`, so a fully-populated, freshly-refreshed table
  can consist entirely of rows the DEFAULT `--period weekly` cannot match — and that answered
  with a byte-identical `count: 0` / `shown=0  (no candidates)` to a healthy table nothing
  qualified in, under a fresh `build_stamp` saying all was well. Two prior passes rejected the
  adjacent finding partly ON A HEADER THAT DID NOT EXIST ("the new table header now makes the
  '0 of N matched' case visible"). Now `rows` (loaded) and `matched` (post-filter, pre-cap) ride
  in the envelope and in the table header, which also makes truncation visible for the first
  time (`rows=31  matched=30  shown=5`, verified live).
  (4) `query.py` — `_validate_tier` echoed `" 1 , 2 "` back as `"1 , 2"` while `_validate_period`
  returns its fully-stripped value (pinned by its own "normalized, not echoed padded" test), so
  two callers sending the same tier set got byte-different envelopes for an identical candidate
  list. Normalized to the joined tokens.
  (5) `query.py` docstring — still documented the two-key sort the previous pass replaced with
  three, i.e. the patch that fixed determinism left its own stated contract behind.
- (change) CHANGED `tests/trending_candidates/test_surface_parity.py` — the previous pass's
  "every registered tool is recorded" guard matched only the CALL form `@mcp.tool()`, so a tool
  registered with the bare `@mcp.tool` (a spelling FastMCP accepts) was invisible to it:
  mutation-verified, appending a bare-decorated tool left the detected count unchanged at 13
  while the same tool with parentheses took it to 14. A registry guard must not depend on how
  the decorator is spelled. Both spellings now match, pinned by a mutation test.
- (change) CHANGED `planning-artifacts/specs/spec-upstream-discovery/tier-taxonomy.md` — its
  "Operator-surface parameters (CAP-3)" table still listed `--period` as `daily|weekly|monthly`
  and `--tier` as a single value, while `query.py`'s docstring, the CLI's `--help` and the pixi
  task description all cite that table as the authority for the flag domain. Reconciled to the
  shipped domain with an explicit as-built note — the same narration fix this story already did
  for `catalog.yml`.
- (event) Re-verified live: full `kedro-test` 1042 passed / 19 skipped; the isolated
  `pytest tests/trending_candidates tests/mcp -q` subset 89 passed (82 before this pass). Both
  behavioural fixes MUTATION-tested: removing the `flush()` fails the two new pipe tests,
  removing the `sys.stdout is sys.__stdout__` guard fails the fd test. Live against a seeded
  31-row parquet: the table header reads `rows=31  matched=30  shown=5`, `--json` round-trips
  through a strict parser with integral counts still emitted as ints (`1029`, `7`, `null`), and
  `env -u PYTHONUNBUFFERED … --json | head -c 0` now exits 1 with EMPTY stderr (it printed
  "Exception ignored while flushing sys.stdout" before this pass). Seeded data removed
  afterwards; the worktree carries no untracked files. Baseline scoped-stamped to this spec
  only.

- (note) Story 32.5 (spec-fleet-consistency-standard CAP-2): the fleet converged on one test-suite vocabulary -- tests/unit/ + tests/integration/ + tests/meta/ (BMAD 6.12's Python default plus the one addition with 8/8 adoption), with fixtures/ and _support/ never collected. - (change) Fleet consistency pass 2026-09-08: fixed a PRE-EXISTING intermittent failure in tests/integration/dashboard/test_identity_catalog_page.py::test_identity_catalog_parity_with_write_canvas. It grouped canvas rows by bucket and compared `canvas_rows[0]` against `loader_df.loc[...].iloc[0]`, which only agrees when the canvas ordering and the parquet/pandas ordering coincide WITHIN a bucket; the fixture puts two records in P2 (`beta-pkg`, `gamma-pkg`), so a differing order failed the name assertion. Observed once in six full-suite runs, passing in isolation every time. Unrelated to any code change -- the test reads no repo files, only tmp_path and a hardcoded fixture. Now pairs the two sides by package name, which is order-independent and checks every row rather than one per bucket; 6/6 repeats plus a full suite (1735 passed) green. RECONCILES: src/shared/packages/pyforge-atlas/tests/integration/dashboard/test_identity_catalog_page.py. Scoped-stamped: --write-baseline --spec pyforge-atlas/spec-pyforge-atlas.
- (change by claude) Fleet consistency pass 2026-09-08 (follow-up): fixed a PRE-EXISTING flaky failure in tests/integration/dashboard/test_dashboard_e2e.py, the sibling defect to the test_identity_catalog_page.py entry above. The `dashboard_server` fixture started the Vizro server in a background process, slept a fixed `time.sleep(3.0)`, then yielded the URL; Vizro builds every page before werkzeug binds the socket, which on this host takes ~5s, so Playwright's first `page.goto` hit `net::ERR_CONNECTION_REFUSED` and the werkzeug "Running on ..." line only appeared in captured TEARDOWN output -- the signature of a URL handed out before anything was listening. Measured 3/3 red on the fix branch under test AND 3/3 red on `main`, with one green outlier across four main runs: the flake favours failure, which is why a single green control run first read as a branch regression. Replaced the sleep with `wait_for_server(port, proc, timeout=60.0)`, which polls `connect_ex` until the port accepts, raises by name if the server process dies before binding (instead of waiting out the timeout on a dead process), and bounds the wait at 60s. 3/3 green after, at ~14.7s per run against the old ~9.9s fast-fail -- direct confirmation the server needed longer than the retired 3.0s. No dashboard, pipeline or product code changed; the fixture is the only edit. RECONCILES: src/shared/packages/pyforge-atlas/tests/integration/dashboard/test_dashboard_e2e.py. Scoped-stamped: `--write-baseline --spec pyforge-atlas/spec-pyforge-atlas`.

- (direction by claude) 2026-09-28 update from docs/dreams/pyforge-atlas.md Realization-log entry '2026-09-28 (night) -- Proposed: Atlas keeps each scanned repo's dependency history, so a fix knows when a dependency arrived and which other repos carry it' (source: the Warden Dream's entry of the same day, the security-scanning intake archive/docs/intake/system_architecture_specification.md, whose Phase 2 persists a dependency_graphs table per repo and commit). What exists: Atlas's datasets cover all of conda-forge (core_dependencies, the sbom_* entry pipeline, the OSV offline store); nothing holds one repo's manifests across its commits, and nothing holds a fleet of enterprise repos. The tool: git-pkgs (MIT; v0.20.0, 2026-09-04) walks a repo's history into SQLite (git pkgs init), with two caveats -- it installs git hooks by default (--no-hooks skips them) and stores its database in the repo's .git by default (GIT_PKGS_DB moves it); its manifests parser has no pixi.toml / pixi.lock support (upstream issue #79), and pixi is the estate's main format. Measured 2026-09-28 against the spine: AD-4 grep-gates any sqlite3 import outside the retired legacy tree (F1), AD-2 puts every source behind a catalog dataset with per-host credential scoping, AD-3 puts a new signal in its assigned pipeline, orchestration/event_source.py is the dagster-free poll-cursor half (webhooks rejected, :17-18), and the Consistency Conventions fix join keys (never a purl), epoch-second timestamps and the stale / unresolved / not-applicable vocabulary. Atlas's PRD and spine were dated 2026-09-20, so this CAP's mint reconciles both in the same change.
- (decision by operator) 2026-09-28 rulings carried from the Warden Dream's entry (final; recorded, not re-asked): Warden scans the enterprise fleet on GitHub Enterprise (spec-pyforge-warden:CAP-26) and its actuator and fleet scan read each repo's dependency history, which Atlas keeps -- the data belongs in Atlas and the verdict in Warden (atlas domain research 2026-07-25; this Spec's 'Atlas measures; Warden judges'). git-pkgs arrives as a conda package (mason Story 21.1, same day). No hook is written into any scanned repo. Webhooks are rejected as the trigger (orchestration/event_source.py:17-18): a poll cursor drives the refresh. The capability carries a flag block (spec-feature-flag-governance:CAP-1, rule date 2026-09-28).
- (decision by claude) 2026-09-28, how the history lands without bending an AD: (a) AD-4 -- git-pkgs' SQLite is a transient raw input, not an Atlas store: GIT_PKGS_DB points at a throwaway path outside the scanned repo, the rows are read through DuckDB (the only engine; its sqlite reader, provisioned offline) or through git-pkgs' own JSON output, never an sqlite3 import, landed as partitioned Parquet, and the SQLite file is deleted after landing, so the F1 grep gate and 'no lingering dual SQLite/Parquet store' hold as written; (b) the estate pixi parser lives in Atlas (tomllib, yaml.safe_load over each revision of pixi.toml / pixi.lock), never an import of pyforge.warden's extractors -- Atlas's one cross-package code dependency stays the optional [gate] extra; (c) the repo list is Warden's fleet inventory export read as a raw catalog dataset (AD-2), never an import of pyforge.warden or django_warden_fabric; (d) the history joins the vcs_health pipeline (AD-3: a new signal joins its assigned pipeline) keyed on repo full name plus pypi_name / conda_name / the native ecosystem name, never a purl; (e) the sensor is event_source.py's shape -- the cursor is each repo's default-branch head SHA from the inventory, the default production source is offline, a moved head triggers the existing job once (coalesced), and the job's refresh is incremental (AD-5); (f) an unreachable repo keeps its last-good partition with a stale marker (AD-13).
- (capability by claude) CAP-61 -- Atlas keeps each scanned repo's dependency history. Intent: Atlas holds, for every repo Warden scans, the history of its declared dependencies across commits -- when each arrived, changed and left, and in which manifest -- so Warden's actuator and fleet scan can see when a vulnerable dependency arrived and which other repos carry it. A primary-layer Parquet dataset in the vcs_health pipeline is built per repo from git pkgs init --no-hooks, with its database at a throwaway path outside the repo and read through DuckDB or git-pkgs' JSON output (never an sqlite3 import, AD-4), plus an Atlas-native estate pixi parser for pixi.toml and pixi.lock, which git-pkgs does not parse (upstream issue #79). No hook or file is ever written into a scanned repo. The repo list is Warden's fleet inventory export (spec-pyforge-warden:CAP-26), read as a catalog dataset, never an import. A poll-cursor sensor in the orchestration/event_source.py pattern -- no webhook -- triggers the refresh when a repo's head moves; the refresh is incremental (AD-5), and an unreachable repo keeps its last-good partition marked stale (AD-13). Atlas measures and Warden judges: the dataset carries no verdict, score or threshold. git-pkgs arrives as a conda package (mason Story 21.1) under a tested version range. (Operator ruling 2026-09-28.) Success: a fixture git repo whose commits add, bump and remove a dependency in pyproject.toml and add one in pixi.toml yields one row per change, with the commit SHA, the epoch-second commit time, the manifest path, the ecosystem, the name, the requirement before and after, and a source naming git-pkgs or the estate pixi parser; after a build the fixture repo's file listing and .git/hooks are unchanged and no pkgs.sqlite3 remains anywhere; no sqlite3 import exists under src/pyforge/atlas (the F1 grep gate); a sensor tick with unchanged heads skips and one with a moved head yields one run request and advances the cursor; an unreachable inventory repo keeps its last-good partition with a stale marker; kedro-test and kedro-catalog-check green.
- (event by claude) Self-validate 2026-09-28 (CAP-61), pass 1 -- structure and coherence: heading, intent and success present (the capability-ledger extractor reads all three); the ID is unique and sequential after CAP-60 (checked against SPEC.md, this memlog and docs/foundry/capability-ledger.yaml: no CAP-61 anywhere before this pass); every success clause is a fixture test, a grep gate or the station's two verify commands. Coherent with the Constraints and spine: 'Atlas measures; Warden judges' (no verdict, score or threshold in the dataset); the DAG is the source of truth and AD-1's import ban holds (the sensor's decision half stays dagster-free, only orchestration/definitions.py wraps it); AD-2 (catalog-owned IO, credentials per host); AD-3 (joins vcs_health); AD-4 and the Non-goal 'Continued SQLite' (git-pkgs' SQLite is a transient raw input read through DuckDB or JSON, never an sqlite3 import, deleted after landing); AD-5 (incremental refresh in the dataset); AD-13 (skip-and-mark-stale); the Consistency Conventions (repo full name plus pypi_name / conda_name / native name as join keys, never a purl; epoch seconds). The frozen exit-code convention is untouched. SPEC.md rendered by an anchor-asserting scratchpad script (operator-approved 2026-09-28; the pre-shell hook refuses Edit/Write on SPEC.md): the CAP block after CAP-60 and updated: 2026-09-28. Status stays ready.
- (event by claude) Self-validate 2026-09-28 (CAP-61), pass 2 -- fidelity to this memlog: every load-bearing claim of the Dream entry, the carried rulings and the landing decision is in SPEC.md -- a per-repo dataset from git pkgs init --no-hooks, the estate pixi parser where git-pkgs has none (issue #79), no hook written into a scanned repo, the join to Warden's fleet inventory (read as data, never an import), the poll-cursor sensor in the event_source.py pattern with no webhook, Warden's actuator and fleet scan as the readers, data in Atlas and the verdict in Warden, git-pkgs as a conda package from mason Story 21.1, the AD-4 landing (no sqlite3 import, transient database), AD-5 and AD-13. The flag block lives on the two story specs (spec-feature-flag-governance:CAP-1), not in the CAP. Nothing in SPEC.md goes beyond the memlog.

- (decision by claude) 2026-10-11, live-attach fixture socket path (Story 28.1) evidence and design. Owner: this Spec. scripts/.spec-surface-baseline.json names spec-pyforge-atlas and spec-pyforge-unifying-strategy as governors of src/shared/packages/pyforge-atlas/tests/unit/test_read_only_live_attach.py; the station that owns the code owns the chain, so atlas carries the fix. Binding: CAP-6 (one engine for compute, graph and vector: the read-only ATTACH joins a Postgres into the one DuckDB plane and never mints a second writable file). The attach itself shipped with steward Story 34.1 under spec-pyforge-unifying-strategy CAP-19 (canopy:FR-46). Defect: fixture_postgres (:88-:209) passes -k '<tmp_path>/pgsock' to pg_ctl; under pr-preflight's per-lane --basetemp the socket path passes the 107-byte AF_UNIX limit, PostgreSQL logs 'Unix-domain socket path ... is too long (maximum 107 bytes)' then 'FATAL: could not create any Unix-domain sockets', and the two fixture-backed tests (test_federated_read_across_two_schemas, test_write_through_attach_is_refused) error at setup. Evidence: warden 16.1's fix pass (pr-preflight -- --keep-going, 2026-10-11) red the pyforge-atlas-test lane with these 2 errors; reproduced on 2b5e0d649b with a 214-byte basetemp and platform-dev's PostgreSQL 17.11: 5 passed, 2 errors; a short basetemp passes 7. Design, proven on a scratch copy (the tracked file is untouched): start the server with -c unix_socket_directories='' in place of -k and drop sock_dir. pg_ctl start and stop read postmaster.pid, and pg_isready, psql and the DSN all use 127.0.0.1, so no socket is needed: 7 passed under the same basetemp, the server log shows only the IPv4 listener, and with no PostgreSQL binaries the 2 tests skip with the existing reason. Alternative, also 7 passed: a short tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix='pgs', dir='/tmp') socket dir removed in finally. Not the default: it still depends on a path length, needs cleanup on every early exit, and must pass dir= explicitly because pr-preflight sets TMPDIR to the lane's long scratch dir. No CAP, no FR, no flag (spec-feature-flag-governance Q1: a fix), no SPEC.md change. Operator ask, verbatim: 'Do this task here: Fix atlas live-attach test socket path overflow'.
- (event by claude) 2026-10-11: Story 28.1 minted in Epic 28 (new; fix, S, deps none). Key 28-1-the-live-attach-fixture-postgres-starts-under-any-basetemp at backlog, epic-28 at backlog and epic-28-retrospective at optional, through a worktree-local Tier-3 feed and sprint-ledger-sync --project atlas. Spec spec-28-1-the-live-attach-fixture-postgres-starts-under-any-basetemp.md: status ready-for-dev, no landing_review, no flag (spec-feature-flag-governance Q1: a fix). ACs: under a basetemp longer than 107 bytes, with PostgreSQL binaries present, the two fixture-backed tests pass, not skip; the fixture opens no Unix socket (-c unix_socket_directories='' in place of -k, no .s.PGSQL.* under the basetemp); with no PostgreSQL binaries both still skip with the existing reason; no skip or xfail added; only the test file changes; restoring -k under tmp_path reds both tests (mutation); kedro-test and kedro-catalog-check green; the spec-pyforge-atlas and spec-pyforge-unifying-strategy memlogs reconcile the test file before two scoped stamps. A new epic because no atlas epic owns the test: steward Story 34.1 shipped it under spec-pyforge-unifying-strategy CAP-19, and atlas mints none of that CAP's stories (epics.md, Validation note 2026-08-26). marshal-policy.toml gains [epic_surfaces]."28"; test-architecture.md is regenerated; epics.md updated: bumps to 2026-10-11. No CAP, FR or AD moves, so SPEC.md, the PRD and the spine are not re-stamped.
- (event) Surface reconcile 2026-10-11 (Story 28.1): src/shared/packages/pyforge-atlas/tests/unit/test_read_only_live_attach.py — fixture_postgres starts PostgreSQL with -c unix_socket_directories='' instead of -k under tmp_path so long pr-preflight basetemp stays under the AF_UNIX path limit; TCP-only comment at pg_ctl options.
- (decision by claude) 2026-10-13, range-test recorder race (Story 29.1) evidence and design. Owner: this Spec (CAP-16, the read surface runs with no backend at all; Epic 8 Story 8.2 shipped the Range proof). src/shared/packages/pyforge-atlas/tests/integration/publish/test_emit_range.py's _RangeServer handler records each response (codes, ranges, range_spans, bytes_served) only after self.wfile.write; the client can finish its query and the test can assert before the server thread runs those appends. Seen 2026-10-13 in a pre-push pr-preflight pyforge-atlas-test lane: test_count_star_reads_footer_only_over_range, 'assert (200 not in [] and 206 in [])'. Reproduced deterministically by delaying the handler 0.5 s after each body write: [] right after the query, [206] a second later. Ruled out first: DuckDB's external file cache across connections on a reused port (fresh connections each issued their own Range request). Fix: record before writing, in every branch (206, 200, 416); a regression test delays the write and still sees the request. A fix story, so no CAP, no FR, no flag; test-only, one file; no skip, xfail, retry or sleep added (AD-11).

- (capability by claude) 2026-10-14: mint CAP-62 (amends CAP-41, Kedro-Viz publish stays in sync, whose success presumed a producer that commits the export; works with CAP-16, the read surface runs with no backend at all; reads pyforge-steward:CAP-166, the CI-surfaces registry and the ci duty) -- the DAG export reaches Pages as a workflow artifact. intent: `.github/workflows/kedro-viz-publish.yml` keeps its path filter (pipelines/**, conf/base/catalog.yml, conf/base/globals.yml, datasets/**) and workflow_dispatch, materializes the stub conf/local/credentials.yml as today, runs `viz-publish-stage`, and uploads the staged tree as the workflow artifact `kedro-viz-export` with 90-day retention instead of calling `steward deploy dashboard`; no step commits or pushes, the re-attach-to-main and git-identity steps go, and the job's permissions.contents drops to read. `viz-publish-stage` writes manifest.json beside index.html: export_commit (the 40-hex commit the DAG was read from: GITHUB_SHA when set, else `git rev-parse HEAD`), export_date (that commit's committer date, ISO 8601 UTC) and kedro_viz_version (equal to the version field of build/api/deploy-viz-metadata); tools/normalize_viz_build.py still strips the checkout path and the wall-clock timestamp, so two builds of one DAG still agree. The job carries the CI-pause fragment reading vars.PAUSE_KEDRO_VIZ_PUBLISH in the shape steward Story 99.2 rendered on every other job; the registry row kedro-viz-publish (steward's, docs/governance/ci-surfaces.yaml) names that switch through steward's co-governor reconcile, and its move from paused-manually to live is the operator's, through steward's ci duty, once the hand-disabled workflow is re-enabled on GitHub. The consumer is steward Story 102.4 on the renamed pages.yml (steward 102.2): it downloads the newest successful kedro-viz-export into /dashboard/kedro-viz/, skips the mount and hides the tile when none exists, and shows the manifest's export_commit and export_date on that page, which keeps its URL. Flag pyforge.atlas.viz_export_artifact; off, viz-publish-stage stages as today and writes no manifest, and the workflow's upload step is skipped. success: on the story's tree `git ls-files docs/dashboard/kedro-viz | wc -l` prints 0 and `git check-ignore docs/dashboard/kedro-viz/index.html` exits 0; `pixi run -e pyforge-atlas viz-publish-stage` with the flag on leaves docs/dashboard/kedro-viz/manifest.json whose three keys parse, export_commit matches ^[0-9a-f]{40}$ and equals `git rev-parse HEAD`, export_date parses as ISO 8601 UTC, and kedro_viz_version equals deploy-viz-metadata's version; with the flag off no manifest.json is written and the staged tree is byte-identical to today's; a workflow test reads kedro-viz-publish.yml as YAML and asserts one actions/upload-artifact step with name kedro-viz-export, retention-days 90 and the staged path, no step naming `steward deploy dashboard`, `git checkout -B main` or `git config user.name`, permissions.contents equal to read, the path filter and workflow_dispatch unchanged, the stub-credentials step present, and the job's if: carrying PAUSE_KEDRO_VIZ_PUBLISH; the registry row kedro-viz-publish names switch PAUSE_KEDRO_VIZ_PUBLISH; docs/dashboard/README.md names the artifact and no longer names `steward deploy dashboard`; `kedro-test` and `kedro-catalog-check` green; the flag test runs the key in both states with pyforge.testing_kit.flags.flag_states.
- (capability by claude) 2026-10-14: mint CAP-63 (extends CAP-8, CAP-40, CAP-42 and CAP-50, the Vizro board pages, and CAP-62, the artifact handoff; binds the steward spine's sld:AD-10 through the refusal `steward deploy static` already applies) -- Vizro boards publish as static exports under /dashboard/vizro/. intent: a `vizro-static-export` task in -e local-recipes (the env that carries the vizro closure; pyforge-guild has none) builds the same BSL-driven Dashboard object `dashboard-dryrun` builds (pyforge.atlas.dashboard.app.build_dashboard, 34 pages, no server, no .run()) and writes one static page per board under docs/dashboard/vizro/<page-id>/index.html and a board index at docs/dashboard/vizro/index.html. The producer job (a second job on kedro-viz-publish.yml, or its own workflow with its own registry row, the operator's call) runs in -e local-recipes, is path-filtered on dashboard/**, semantic/** and conf/base/catalog.yml, and uploads docs/dashboard/vizro/ as the workflow artifact vizro-static-export (90-day retention); the Pages build consumes it as it consumes kedro-viz-export (steward 102.4's step; herald CAP-61 M3 mounts it through publicDir), and pages-check judges the mount with no VENDORED_ROOTS exemption in docsite/tools/assemble_pages.py. The registry docs/governance/ci-surfaces.yaml, its schema and the steward ci duty are steward's; atlas names the switch and the row, and the paused-manually to live move is the operator's. pages.yml / dashboard.yml are steward's and herald's: atlas changes only its own producer workflow. The committed export leaves git; the staging path is gitignored, never tracked again.
- (decision by claude) 2026-10-14, CAP-62 design, with evidence. Why an artifact and not the commit: the committed export (docs/dashboard/kedro-viz/, 11 MB, 215 tracked files) was last written 2026-08-15 while 23 first-parent merges since 2026-08-24 touched the watched paths, so CAP-41's 'stays in sync' has not held for two months; the commit path needs contents: write, a re-attached main, a bot identity and steward's reconciled push, all of which an upload-artifact step removes (the intake's finding: the bot-commit pattern and the stuck-in-waiting incident share one root, a deploy decoupled from its cause; M2's done-when is zero github-actions[bot] commits on main). Why 90 days: the herald Dream's M2 names kedro-viz-export at 90-day retention; the consumer tolerates absence, so an expired artifact hides the tile rather than failing the build. Why a manifest: the normalizer sets deploy-viz-metadata's timestamp to 'unset' so builds are reproducible, which also erased the one date the page could show; manifest.json restores provenance as data the consumer renders (AD-17), without patching kedro-viz's index.html. export_commit comes from GITHUB_SHA when set so the manifest names the commit the runner checked out, and from git rev-parse HEAD locally. Why the normalizer stays: the checkout-path strip keeps the export free of runner paths, which matters as much in an artifact as in a commit. Why the staging path stays docs/dashboard/kedro-viz/ and is gitignored: the assembler's VENDORED_ROOTS and the local pages-build read it there, so a developer's local run and the consumer's download agree on one path; only the tracking changes. Why the stub-credentials step stays: kedro viz build still resolves the whole catalog and two entries declare credentials. CAP-63 design: the exporter reuses build_dashboard so the static boards are the same object the live server runs (the intake's unaddressed drift risk closed by construction); one vendored plotly.min.js via a relative include_plotlyjs path rather than plotly's 'directory' mode, which writes a copy per output directory; the per-page AD-17 stamp becomes the manifest's data_build_timestamp because it already exists and is already proven; -e local-recipes because the vizro closure is not in pyforge-guild (dashboard-dryrun already runs there); the export refuses a declared access column before writing because steward's deploy static does and sld:AD-10 says refuse, never warn. Architecture: atlas AD-21 (WASM read surface: static Parquet + HTTP Range, zero backend; binds the published artifact layout, host-agnostic emitter, Pages the default host) is amended, not a new AD: both capabilities are static surfaces atlas publishes with zero backend to the same host, and the satellites already hold AD-24..AD-56, so a new primary AD would read a fifty-seventh AD. Chain placement: FR-70 (CAP-62) and FR-71 (CAP-63), the PRD's next free ids after FR-69; one epic, Epic 30, two stories, 30.1 (M) then 30.2 (L, Deps S-30.1).
- (decision by claude) 2026-10-14, flag class for Stories 30.1 and 30.2 under spec-feature-flag-governance: both are type feature behind a flag, not exempt. 30.1 adds an upload path and a manifest to a workflow and a task (pyforge.atlas.viz_export_artifact; off, viz-publish-stage stages as today, no manifest, the upload step skipped); 30.2 adds an export path and a producer artifact (pyforge.atlas.vizro_static_boards; off, the task refuses and writes nothing). Neither is docs-only (both change behaviour), detector-or-gate (neither judges), flag-infrastructure, recipe-build or planning-ledger-only. Tree default off; overlay dev on, staging off, production off, so the public Pages deploy keeps today's shape until the operator flips the key; cleanup 90 days after on in every environment (Q4). Readers go through pyforge.core.flags.read_boolean (steward 75.1's contract) and the flag gate counts them because they live under src/ (the exporter) and are handed to the pixi task and the workflow step as resolved values.
- (decision by claude) 2026-10-14, CAP-62 and CAP-63 non-goals: no atlas step commits to main, calls deploy-pages or edits pages.yml / dashboard.yml (steward's and herald's); no change to which surfaces exist in docs/governance/ci-surfaces.yaml beyond the kedro-viz-publish row's switch, and that through steward's reconcile; no retirement of kedro-viz-publish.yml (it is the producer); no patch to kedro-viz's own index.html to show the commit (the consumer renders the manifest); no second deploy caller and no release-asset zip (M2's, steward's); no live Vizro, filters emulated in the browser, or data for every state embedded in a page; no new atlas CLI verb or MCP tool for the export (a pixi task is the entry; a verb is a later CAP if wanted); no change to the 34-page inventory or to DESIGN.md's controls; no move of /dashboard/kedro-viz/ to /pipelines/ (the placement rule keeps its URL; a rename is herald's M3 call).
- (assumption by claude) 2026-10-14, CAP-62 and CAP-63: actions/upload-artifact and actions/download-artifact are available on every Pages host the one artifact deploys to (herald CAP-56's second host included); not checked live. The consumer (steward 102.4) tolerates an absent or expired artifact, so a 90-day gap hides the tile and never reds the Pages build. As landed on 2026-10-14, steward's run_resume accepts only a row whose state is paused and which names one switch (ci.py:1067-1090), and the kedro-viz-publish row reads paused-manually with switch null; the move to live therefore needs steward's row edit and the operator re-enabling the hand-disabled workflow on GitHub before or instead of ci resume. No Vizro page declares an access column today (no AccessDeclaration or access_column under pyforge/atlas/dashboard/), so sld:AD-10's refusal is proven on a fixture board, not on a live one.
- (question by claude) 2026-10-14, CAP-62: which duty moves the kedro-viz-publish registry row from paused-manually to live once the workflow carries PAUSE_KEDRO_VIZ_PUBLISH, given run_resume refuses a row that is not paused: a steward story that lets ci resume accept paused-manually when the workflow is enabled again, or an operator hand edit of the row with gh workflow enable? Operator's call; named as 30.1's operator gate until then. CAP-63: does the Vizro producer run as a second job on kedro-viz-publish.yml (one atlas producer workflow, one registry row) or as its own workflow with its own row (one owner per workflow holds either way)? Operator's call before 30.2 dispatches; the story spec is written for the second job and names the alternative.

This story is one of the three: DW-atlas-steward-100-7-1 (Atlas fails mypy in pyforge-foundry-full, so sbom-laptop-gate is red on main; owner atlas, severity medium). Scope relayed with it: (a) atlas passes mypy in pyforge-foundry-full through typed call sites against the real library signatures, with per-module, per-error-code [[tool.mypy.overrides]] only where an error cannot be typed (the 2026-09-20 baseline rule), never ignore_errors; (b) a lane runs that foundry-full mypy leg so it cannot drift again. - (decision by claude) 2026-10-14, evidence and design for atlas mypy in pyforge-foundry-full (Story 31.1). Owner: this Spec, CAP-20 (one command runs every check CI runs, type checking included) and AD-32 (every gate is a named task; local and CI never drift apart). The lane is spec-pyforge-steward:CAP-153 (lint and types gate the ten pyforge-* packages) and its list spec-pyforge-steward:CAP-159; sbom-laptop-gate is fnd:CAP-13. Measured at 846d8383f5 in a fresh worktree: pixi run --frozen -e pyforge-foundry-full lint-types exits 1 at its mypy leg; pyforge-atlas reports Found 72 errors in 8 files (checked 130 source files); the other nine pyforge-* packages, ruff and ruff-format pass; the same mypy (2.4.0, the lock) over pyforge-guild reports none, because pyforge-guild installs none of Vizro, Dash, Dagster, Kedro, OpenLineage, Bokeh, BeautifulSoup or DuckDB and ignore_missing_imports reads them as Any. The primary checkout env is behind the lock on mypy alone (2.3.1) and reports the same 72. By file: dashboard/app.py 59 (54 call-arg, 4 arg-type, 1 operator), views/widgets.py 4 attr-defined, datasets/upstream_discovery.py 2 (union-attr, call-overload), orchestration/definitions.py 2 arg-type, query_plane_vectors.py 2 arg-type, mcp/session.py 1 misc, observability.py 1 arg-type, rag/store.py 1 index. The DW row per-file counts summed to 79 and named FastHTML; the Nav is Dash html.Nav. The 54 call-arg all read Missing named argument on a Vizro model (Page, Card, AgGrid, Graph, Action, UserInput, Button, Text, LandmarkDashboard) whose default sits inside Annotated[..., Field(default=...)], which mypy reads as required. The pydantic mypy plugin, in a throwaway config, clears 10 of the 54 (Card.href, AgGrid.footer, declared = Field(...)) and the arg-type at app.py:524, and leaves 44. Passing the defaults explicitly changes model_fields_set, which Vizro reads (an explicit Action.inputs selects its deprecated legacy path), so those 54 cannot be typed at the call site. One error is a real defect: observability.py:272 passes the outputStatistics facet under OutputDataset facets=, but OutputStatisticsOutputDatasetFacet is an OutputDatasetFacet, which openlineage-python 1.53.0 takes under outputFacets; four assertions in tests/unit/observability/test_observability_fixtures.py (:120, :229, :241, :295) pin the wrong key. No workflow runs mypy in pyforge-foundry-full: lint-types.yml runs only pixi run --frozen -e pyforge-guild lint-types, and no workflow runs sbom-laptop-gate. Design, proven on a scratch copy (nothing committed): (1) typed fixes for the 17 non-call-arg errors and the one the Bokeh import fix exposes (views/widgets.py:158, Select options): the Page controls element type; isinstance narrowing of model_manager[grid_id] to vm.AgGrid; a dict[str, Any] spread for Dash aria-* wildcard props; Bokeh widgets imported from bokeh.models.widgets.inputs and .tables; a str check on the href attribute and find(None, {...}); the dependency mapping type graph.dependencies carries and a job parameter widened to the UnresolvedAssetJobDefinition define_asset_job returns (typing-only imports); LockedDuckDB accepted by rag/store.py load_vss_offline, VssLoader and DuckdbVssRagStore connection, so query_plane_vectors.py does not change; KedroSession.create bound before with session, keeping KedroSession.__exit__; a None check before fetchone()[0]. (2) outputStatistics moves to outputFacets and the four assertions follow it. (3) One override: call-arg on pyforge.atlas.dashboard.app. With those, mypy reports no issues against both interpreters; the unit and meta suites and the dashboard integration tests fail exactly the same environment-bound tests on the fixed and an untouched scratch copy; restoring facets= fails 2 tests. No inline type: ignore: warn_unused_ignores turns one that foundry-full needs into an unused-ignore error in pyforge-guild. (4) Coverage: atlas touched-module floor is 80 percent unit; at 846d8383f5 with no vss extension provisioned, mcp/session.py is 75.0 (bootstrapped_session body never runs), rag/store.py 35.1 and query_plane_vectors.py 30.9 (vss tests skip). Scratch tests lifted rag/store.py to 96.5 (a real in-memory DuckDB with only the three vss-owned statements dropped) and mcp/session.py to 100. (5) The lane: the existing mypy task (scripts/lint_types.py mypy) runs unchanged in pyforge-foundry-full, which composes guild-tasks; ten packages took 58 s cold and 2.4 s warm; a guild and a foundry-full run sharing one cache, started concurrently three times, each gave its own correct verdict. It becomes a second step of the lint-types CI job (one job, one setup-pixi pin, so no ci-surfaces row and no pixi_version_registry change) and a pr-preflight-lanes entry, { task = "mypy", environment = "pyforge-foundry-full" }; an atlas meta test pins both. Co-governors the implementation reconciles: spec-pyforge-steward (lint-types.yml); the seven pixi.toml governors; spec-pyforge-core and spec-pyforge-unifying-strategy (atlas src and tests).

- (decision by claude) 2026-10-17, atlas Story 32.1 (a fix under CAP-62; Dream entry 2026-10-17, kedro-viz-publish's stub credentials drifted): kedro-viz-publish.yml run 39002722593 (workflow_dispatch, 990d796c7f) failed in its publish job with 'Unable to find credentials gitlab_token': the workflow's own stub step wrote 2 of the 8 credential keys conf/base/catalog.yml names, tools/bootstrap.py's stub (a second copy) wrote all 8, and kedro-viz 12.5.0 exits 0 on a failed build, so viz-build read green. Decided: (1) tools/bootstrap.py's CREDENTIAL_KEYS is the one list, every key written {} (measured on a scratch copy: an all-{} stub and an all-pair stub both build, so the workflow comment's 'APIDataset entries need a pair' does not hold); the workflow's stub step runs tools/bootstrap.py --credentials-only and carries no key list; the guard is _ensure_credentials_stub's (absent or zero-byte file only). (2) tests/unit/test_viz_publish_build.py fails when CREDENTIAL_KEYS and the catalog's credentials: values differ, or when a workflow run: names a key or writes credentials.yml. (3) viz-build runs tools/viz_build_check.py after kedro viz build and exits 1 unless build/api/main, build/index.html and build/api/deploy-viz-metadata exist. CAP-62's success clause 'the stub-credentials step unchanged' is the clause this fix moves: the step stays, named and placed as before, and its content now comes from tools/bootstrap.py; SPEC.md is not hand-edited here, and the next bmad-spec re-derive of CAP-62 takes that wording. No CAP, FR, AD or flag.

Should-fix 1: the publish job's 'Resolve viz export artifact flag' step ran tools/print_viz_export_flag.py in -e pyforge-guild, which does not carry pyforge.atlas (ModuleNotFoundError, exit 1; enabled=true in -e pyforge-atlas), so past a green build the job would still fail before the upload; latent since Story 30.1, since no run had reached the step. Orchestrator ruling 2026-10-17: fold it into 32.1, do not mint 32.2. The step now runs in -e pyforge-atlas, the env the job's setup-pixi installs; Story 32.1's AC6 is amended from 'the flag gate byte-identical' to 'byte-identical except its environment', and a test fails when any pixi-run atlas tool step in the workflow names another env. CAP-62's intent (the job uploads kedro-viz-export when the flag is on) is unchanged. Should-fix 2 and the nits are test hardening and wording (no count of keys in prose). No CAP, FR, AD or flag.

- (decision) 2026-10-18, atlas Story 33.1 (a fix under CAP-9, agents trigger and read the pipeline natively; Dream entry 2026-10-18, the atlas CLI has no verbs): pyforge.atlas.__main__.main hands every argument to kedro's find_run_command, which returns the run command itself, so 'pyforge atlas run --pipeline core' (the SKF skill's quick start, the bmad-agent-atlas RUN menu) exits 2 with 'Got unexpected extra argument (run)', and ipython/package are unreachable. The FR-155 parity gate (mcp/parity.py, Story 24.3) derives its verbs from kedro.framework.cli.project.project_group and TOOL_SPECS declares ['run','--pipeline',<name>], so both described a CLI the binary never had; the gate checks verb existence, not reachability. From the repo root a run also resolves its Kedro project to the cwd (find_kedro_project returns None), reading the repo root's conf/ instead of atlas's. Operator ruling 2026-10-18 (AskUserQuestion, verbatim choices): 'Fix the binary (Recommended)' over a docs-only fix, and 'Print help (Recommended)' for a bare 'pyforge atlas'. Decision: main dispatches to kedro's project_group with the atlas project's metadata as obj, after bootstrap_project on and a chdir to the member dir that mcp/session.py PROJECT_ROOT names (the MCP face's root, AD-23); --version stays the first-token intercept; TOOL_SPECS declares --pipelines (kedro 1.6 deprecates --pipeline). A fix: no CAP, no FR, no flag; CAP-9's text does not change.

No pipeline, dataset, catalog entry or MCP tool behaviour changed.
