- (decision) Operator rulings 2026-10-18 (AskUserQuestion, verbatim choices), from the Dream entry of that date (atlas fills its own data): 'Wire the fetchers now'; fetchers live 'In-package, public live (Recommended)' -- public sources go live by default by composing kedro_datasets.api.APIDataset or a subprocess through pyforge.core.process, credentialed sources only on an operator opt-in; VCS population 'Full universe (Recommended)'; extras 'Phase P via ClickHouse, Basilisk vulns, OpenTeams identity sources, Estate-source producer'; order 'Kedro first, legacy after'; opt-in 'Env var per run (Recommended)' = PYFORGE_ATLAS_CREDENTIALED=1; decomposition 'Approve as drafted (Recommended)' (Epic 34 public sources, Epic 35 VCS and registries, Epic 36 extras); SPEC.md render 'Approve script render (Recommended)'. BigQuery stays off (ruling 2026-10-17).
- (decision) 2026-10-18: the AD-13 'default None is offline' seam stance is superseded for public sources: a dataset whose source is public and unauthenticated fetches live by default, in-package; DW-B5-2's Dagster-resource injection is no longer the path for them (the Dagster schedule stays the cadence). The no-inline-IO denylist does not change. AD-11 holds: credentialed runs stay attended, expressed as the per-run opt-in PYFORGE_ATLAS_CREDENTIALED=1.
- (capability) CAP-64 -- a plain run fills every dataset a public source serves: on a fresh data root, pyforge-atlas-bootstrap then pyforge atlas run fills, live and in-package, the split cf-graph repos (conda-forge-bot-data-node_attrs, -pr_info, -version_pr_info, sharded) for dependencies, feedstock health and maintainers; Anaconda's monthly S3 parquet for every download table; the bot's mappings/pypi/name_mapping.yaml for the Phase C mapping; endoflife.date and the SPDX list as parsed records; the vdb, OSV, five OS-distro, trending, about-maintainers, purl-associator, Anaconda-distribution and AOSS stores through refreshers the datasets own; migration status through a driver node; Phase P from the public ClickHouse mirror. Unreachable endpoint keeps last-good and marks stale (AD-13). Companion live-sources.md maps each source to its datasets, legacy phase and parity measure.
- (capability) CAP-65 -- VCS and registry fetches cover the full actionable universe, credentialed on opt-in: GitHub, GitLab, Codeberg and the eight registries draw their population from the conda-forge universe (core enumeration joined with the repo/dev/homepage URLs and registry names node_attrs carries), as legacy Phases K, L and N select it, not the OpenTeams identity subset; upstream versions from releases and tags (Phase K), feedstock health from feedstock repos (E.5, N); GitHub authenticates with a bearer header built from github_token, attached to the GitHub host only (AD-2); every credentialed request runs only when PYFORGE_ATLAS_CREDENTIALED=1 is set for the run, otherwise its store keeps last-good and marks stale.
- (capability) CAP-66 -- the Basilisk population runs: driver nodes call BasiliskBatchDataset.query_population over core_packages_enumerated (conda PURL keys) and BasiliskDetailDataset.fetch_details for matched advisories through a public fetcher; the pre-announcement endpoint degrades per AD-13.
- (capability) CAP-67 -- the OpenTeams identity sources fill on opt-in: the OpenTeams project board (GraphQL) and the staged-recipes PR list (REST) fetch with github_token under PYFORGE_ATLAS_CREDENTIALED=1, beside the public purl associator and about-maintainers lists (CAP-64), so identity_packages_primary, the identity exports and inventory priorities fill.
- (capability) CAP-68 -- the query-plane estate source has a producer: a named atlas node writes query_plane_estate_source (declared by steward Story 34.2, read by query_plane_cache, canopy FR-47 Mode B), so the estate cache builds from data rather than a test fixture.
- (constraint) Public sources live by default, credentialed on opt-in (2026-10-18): no gate, loop path or CI job sets PYFORGE_ATLAS_CREDENTIALED; with it unset no request carries a credential. Gates stay fixture-based and network-free.
- (question) CAP-66: does the Basilisk population run unattended on every plain run (public, about 35 batched requests for the universe) or only on an operator opt-in (DW-B8-3 recorded it as attended)? To settle before Story 36.1.
- (question) CAP-68: which platform OLTP table(s) does the estate source extract (canopy FR-47 Mode B names 'heavy tables'; the model today is a sku/units toy), and through which seam does atlas read them (pyforge.core.station_port, a steward export)? To settle before Story 36.3.

- (decision) Operator rulings 2026-10-19 (AskUserQuestion, verbatim choices), on the Vizro boards (Dream entry 2026-10-19, the Vizro boards read what the pipelines write): 'Data-first + 10 rewires (Recommended)' -- the Pages job builds public data first, the local data root is fixed, the 10 rewire boards are wired now, and the 6 new-node boards become backlog stories with DW rows; 'Public-only Pages (Recommended)' -- CI runs public sources only and the identity boards show their honest-empty notice on Pages; 'Weekly + on push (Recommended)' -- a weekly scheduled public refresh plus the existing push trigger and manual dispatch. - (decision) 2026-10-19, CAP-63 amended: the vizro producer job builds the public atlas data before it exports -- the credentials stub (tools/bootstrap.py --credentials-only), then pyforge atlas run over the public pipelines with no PYFORGE_ATLAS_CREDENTIALED and BigQuery off (AD-11), then vizro-static-export against that data root -- on a weekly schedule as well as its push trigger and workflow_dispatch. The identity and enterprise boards carry no rows on Pages (their sources are credentialed or internal); nothing else in CAP-63 changes.
- (capability) CAP-69 (minted 2026-10-19) -- the Vizro boards read the datasets the pipelines write. intent: dashboard.data.default_data_root resolves the data root Kedro writes (the member's data/, or PYFORGE_ATLAS_DATA_ROOT); each board whose content a pipeline already produces reads that dataset -- the four seed-gap boards from seed_gaps' derived reports, version-downloads from core_version_download_history, distribution-breakdown from the three core_downloads_*_breakdown tables, behind-upstream from vcs_upstream_versions against the enumerated latest version, whodepends from core_dependencies, live-catalog-coverage from the inventory flags, universe-sbom from the universal SBOM -- with each board's dimension names unchanged and only paths and expressions moving; the six boards no pipeline feeds (cve-watcher history, release cadence, find-alternative, export-purls, mapping-gap, bootstrap-index-health) are backlog stories; the on-demand boards stay honest-empty until the tool runs. success: on a data root a public pyforge atlas run wrote, every board among the 12 already wired and the 10 rewired whose dataset holds rows renders a non-empty table in dashboard-dryrun and in the static export; a test per rewired board proves its loader on a fixture shaped like the produced dataset; dashboard-dryrun, the provenance test and kedro-catalog-check stay green.

- (decision) 2026-10-22, Story 37.11 (a board's note reads 'Data gap' only where data is missing; a fix under CAP-63 / CAP-69, no CAP, no flag). Evidence: after Story 37.4 the Pages export shows 21 of 34 boards with data, yet dashboard/app.py labels every PageDef.note '**Data gap:**' (_legibility_card and the identity-workbook page), so behind-upstream's 95 rows sit under 'Data gap: Wired to build_behind_upstream_model...'. Rule: the note reads '**About this board:**' on a page with a data function whose own dataset is available; '**Data gap:**' stays for a shell page (no data function), the identity workbook's gap_message, and a page whose AD-17 provenance is unavailable. dashboard/static_export.py strips the live-scan note under either label. Label only: no board's data or note text changes.

- (decision) 2026-10-23 Story 37.5 (CAP-69, operator go-ahead 'keep moving, be autonomous'): the cve-watcher reads a vulnerability history. A new SnapshotHistoryDataset (append-only Parquet keyed on snapshot_at+conda_name; an absent or unreadable store loads empty and is never overwritten, AD-13) holds vulnerability_rollup_snapshots; snapshot_vulnerability_rollup stamps each run's rollup and diff_vulnerability_snapshots writes derived_vuln_history, comparing the latest snapshot with the most recent one older than params cve_watcher.since_days (7) else the second-latest, as legacy cve_watcher.query does. The board reads it behind the existing flag pyforge.atlas.board_projections (no new flag; off it reads primary/vuln_history as before). Deviations from legacy: severities are total, critical_high and kev (the rollup counts critical and high together); a package absent from a snapshot reads as zero (the rollup has no zero-count rows); no maintainer or EPSS filter. Pages builds from a fresh run, so the board stays empty there until a store persists across runs. Closes DW-atlas-37-5-1.

- (decision) 2026-10-23 Story 37.10 (CAP-69, operator go-ahead 'keep moving, be autonomous'): the bootstrap-index-health reports each catalog entry's freshness. A read-only CatalogIndexDataset reads conf/base/catalog.yml and lists every entry whose filepath lies under the data root with its last write (a store directory's newest Parquet), its Parquet footer row count and its staleness marker; report_catalog_index_health adds the catalog-sources tier (params catalog_index_health.tiers, each source by the dataset that persists it), the scale sanity floor and a status (missing, stale, empty, below_floor, ok) into catalog_index_health. The node lives in a new catalog_index pipeline that pipeline_registry keeps out of __default__ (AFTER_RUN_PIPELINES): a node with no upstream edge would run first in a combined DAG and read the root before the run writes it. The Pages job runs it last; pyforge-atlas-bootstrap is not changed (pixi.toml has eight governing Specs). Not an MCP trigger; no Dagster timeout or job. Behind the existing flag pyforge.atlas.board_projections. Closes DW-atlas-37-10-1.

Summary and keywords come from the cf-graph anchor rows (about.summary, about.keywords, legacy Phase E CFA:2327-2334), added as two additive columns; the archived set is the cf-graph archived flag joined with semantic_packages.feedstock_archived (vcs_archived_feedstocks), as core's Phase J skip-set already reads it. Recency and adoption_stage read semantic_packages' upload columns, which stay NULL until per-version upload times land (Story 37.6): recency is 1.0, as legacy gives a package with no upload time, and the board's adoption_stage is build_packages_model's classifier over the same inputs. Behind pyforge.atlas.board_projections.

- (decision) 2026-10-23 operator ruling, Story 37.6 (CAP-69, release-cadence): per-version upload times come from the full repodata.json.zst per subdir (not the anaconda.org files fan-out). A version's upload time is the earliest file timestamp across every subdir, legacy package_version_downloads.upload_unix's 'min upload_time across files of this version'. The fetch is public, behind pyforge.atlas.live_public_sources, cached per subdir with an ETag and an AD-13 last-good marker, and parsed as a stream (stdlib compression.zstd plus incremental JSON decode), so a Pages runner never holds a whole repodata document.

- (decision) 2026-10-24 Story 34.12 (CAP-64, a fix: no new CAP, no flag; closes DW-atlas-38-1-1): a vulnerability feed that cannot be reached keeps its last good copy. CisaKevDataset, EpssFeedDataset and CweCatalogDataset share one base, VulnerabilityFeedDataset, which fetches on every load as today and keeps the last frame that parsed to rows as <filepath>/last_good.parquet (atomic_write). A fetch failure (no connection, an HTTP error, a timeout) or a body that is not the feed's format or parses to no rows serves that copy, or on a fresh root the empty frame of the feed's columns, under the AD-13 .staleness.json marker, and never raises; a filesystem error on the copy or the marker is logged. Each request carries a 90 s timeout and public_sources.request_args' no-op auth (no ~/.netrc credential), and an archive member is read to at most 256 MiB. The three catalog entries gain filepath ${globals:paths.data_root}/raw/<entry>, so the bootstrap-index-health board (Story 37.10) shows a feed stale with its reason, and provenance reports the copy's age under the marker (AD-17). Evidence: kedro-viz-publish's Vizro job failed on five merges to main on 2026-10-23 with 'vulnerability_cwe_catalog_raw: Failed to connect to the remote server' (runs 39908595513, 39931632612, 39941141589, 40018149689, 40027620039). Epic 34's rule: a fix restores a source meant to work and needs no flag; gating the fetch behind pyforge.atlas.live_public_sources would stop the production overlay (off) fetching these feeds, so the fetch stays unconditional, as Story 34.1's JSONSourceDataset is.
- (event) Surface reconcile 2026-10-24 (Story 34.12, CAP-64; branch atlas-vuln-feed-fallback-2026-10-24; reviews 1-3 PASS, every finding fixed): src/shared/packages/pyforge-atlas/src/pyforge/atlas/datasets/vulnerability_feeds.py (VulnerabilityFeedDataset, the base of CisaKevDataset, EpssFeedDataset and CweCatalogDataset: fetches on every load with a 90 s timeout and the no-op auth; keeps the last frame that parsed to rows and that Parquet can hold as last_good.parquet; a fetch failure, an HTTP error, a malformed or empty body, or an archive member past 32 MiB, read in one bounded read, serves that copy or the empty frame under the AD-13 marker and never raises; a copy it cannot serve is set aside as .last_good.parquet.rejected; refreshed is set only on a load that served what it fetched. This corrects the 256 MiB the decision above names: the cap is 32 MiB), src/shared/packages/pyforge-atlas/src/pyforge/atlas/datasets/__init__.py (exports VulnerabilityFeedDataset), src/shared/packages/pyforge-atlas/src/pyforge/atlas/provenance.py (the base joins the marked datasets, AD-17), src/shared/packages/pyforge-atlas/conf/base/catalog.yml (vulnerability_cisa_kev_raw, vulnerability_epss_raw and vulnerability_cwe_catalog_raw gain filepath raw/<entry>), src/shared/packages/pyforge-atlas/tests/unit/datasets/test_vulnerability_feeds.py (78 tests, no network); the companion live-sources.md gains the three feeds' row. Story 34.12 done; closes DW-atlas-38-1-1.
- (decision) 2026-10-24 Story 37.12 (CAP-63 amended 2026-10-19, CAP-64; a fix: no new CAP, no flag): the Pages Vizro job keeps the public stores' last-good copies between runs. Story 34.12's residual risk: the kedro-viz-publish vizro job builds on a fresh data root every run, so a feed that cannot be reached has no copy to serve and Pages publishes its table empty under the AD-13 marker (the bootstrap-index-health board reads missing). The job now restores the newest saved copy with actions/cache/restore before the build (key pyforge-atlas-last-good-<run_id>, restore-keys pyforge-atlas-last-good-) and saves this run's with actions/cache/save after a build that succeeds. What persists is what a dataset keeps as its own last-good copy under the data root: data/raw (every raw-layer copy and its marker) and data/stores/* except data/stores/vdb (the 9.6 GB vulnerability database store stays rebuilt per run, as today). Pipeline outputs (intermediate, primary, derived) never persist, so a node that stops writing cannot hide behind an old file. The listing is stores/* with !stores/vdb, never stores with !stores/vdb: actions/cache resolves globs with implicitDescendants false and tar recurses into a listed directory, so a child exclusion under a listed parent is ignored. Consequence, as on a workstation: a store inside its refresh cadence, or whose ETag has not changed, is not fetched again. Public-only (AD-11) holds: actions/cache uses the runner's own cache service, no secret and no token appear in the job, PYFORGE_ATLAS_CREDENTIALED is never set; the workflow runs on push to main, schedule and dispatch only, so no pull request can write a cache main restores. A failed build still fails the job and saves nothing; a cache miss or eviction is today's fresh root.
- (decision by operator) 2026-10-24 operator ruling, Story 37.12 (option C, 'Cache + raise limit'): the Pages vizro job keeps the last-good copies in actions/cache, and the operator raises the repository's Actions cache limit themself (an operator-owned setting, never changed from a session). Why: review 1 measured the cache over its 10 GB limit (10,450,545,115 bytes on 2026-10-24) with the least recently used entries evicted after about 3.5 hours unread, so until the limit is raised the copies survive only between runs a few hours apart, never to the weekly run; a miss is the fresh root of before. The alternatives were an artifact hand-off through a sibling job holding github.token with actions: read (as pages.yml does), or a best-effort cache with no limit change. The pending raise is DW-atlas-37-12-1 (operator-owned) and a scribe project capture.
- (event) Correction 2026-10-24 to the Story 37.12 decision entry above (reviews 1 and 2; the memlog is append-only): (1) the cache key is pyforge-atlas-last-good-<run_id>-<run_attempt>, so a successful re-run saves its fresher copies; (2) data/raw and data/stores/* hold the dataset-owned last-good stores and their markers plus five plain raw-layer entries that are no such store (org_audit_candidates, artifactory_downloads_raw and artifactory_consumption_raw, rewritten every build; query_plane_estate_source and warden_fleet_inventory, written only by steward and warden, never by the Pages build), and pypi_conda_map_store merges each export onto its last copy, so on Pages that map grows across runs as on a workstation; only the output layers (intermediate, primary, derived) and cache/ never persist; (3) the cache's trust rests on no workflow in .github/workflows having a trigger that runs untrusted input in main's cache scope (pull_request_target, workflow_run, issue_comment, issues, discussion, discussion_comment), not on this workflow's own triggers: a pull_request run saves to the pull request's own scope. Output-layer stores that read their own previous copy (vulnerability_rollup_snapshots, the IncrementalParquetDataset caches) still start empty on Pages: DW-atlas-37-12-2, blocked on DW-atlas-37-12-1.
- (event) Correction 2026-10-24 (Story 37.12 review 3) to the Story 37.12 entries above: (1) the cache's eviction horizon is not a fixed 3.5 hours; it depends on how much the other workflows write (the 11:42Z entry was still present at 15:54Z, about 4 hours unread); (2) the cache now also keeps data/primary/vulnerability_rollup_snapshots, the SnapshotHistoryDataset the cve-watcher diffs (Story 37.5), because diff_vulnerability_snapshots falls back to the second-latest snapshot when none is since_days old, so two cached snapshots hours apart already fill the cve-watcher on Pages (closes DW-atlas-37-12-2, opened on the wrong premise that a week of survival was needed); (3) what never persists is stores/vdb and every other output-layer file, so a stopped node's table in intermediate/, primary/ or derived/ cannot hide behind an old file; the raw layer's plain node outputs (org_audit_candidates, artifactory_*_raw) are cached and rewritten every build; (4) the IncrementalParquetDataset TTL row caches stay uncached by design (an optimisation; each Pages build recomputes them as before).
- (decision by operator) 2026-10-24 operator ruling, Story 37.12 scope change: 'Approve' (given in the Story 37.12 session) -- the kedro-viz-publish vizro job also keeps the vulnerability snapshot history (data/primary/vulnerability_rollup_snapshots, the SnapshotHistoryDataset the cve-watcher diffs, Story 37.5) between Pages runs beside data/raw and data/stores/*, so the cve-watcher board fills on Pages. Why: diff_vulnerability_snapshots falls back to the second-latest snapshot, so two cached snapshots hours apart already give a diff (Story 37.12 review 3).
- (event) Surface reconcile 2026-10-24 (Story 37.12, CAP-63 / CAP-64; branch claude/laughing-jackson-2bff14; reviews 1-4, every finding fixed or ruled on by the operator): .github/workflows/kedro-viz-publish.yml (the vizro job restores the newest pyforge-atlas-last-good- Actions cache entry before the data build and saves pyforge-atlas-last-good-<run_id>-<run_attempt> after a build that succeeds, both under the build's gate; paths data/raw, data/stores/*, data/primary/vulnerability_rollup_snapshots and, last, !data/stores/vdb; the header and step comments); src/shared/packages/pyforge-atlas/tests/unit/test_vizro_static_boards.py (seven Story 37.12 tests: step order and gate, one path list and key, what persists, the build's own data root, every last-good store and the snapshot history cached with 32 pinned last-good classes and seven raw entries that are not, no workflow running untrusted input in main's cache scope); _bmad-output/projects/pyforge-atlas/planning-artifacts/deferred-work-ledger.md (DW-atlas-37-12-1 operator-owned open: raise the repository's Actions cache limit; DW-atlas-37-12-2 opened and closed on the branch; DW-atlas-37-12-3 open: a failed vdb build republishes the previous cve-watcher diff as current; the sub-row count 209 -> 216). Wording correction to the entries above: two cached snapshots give the cve-watcher a pair to diff from the second run whose cache survives, and a pair that moves no count still renders empty; 'fills the cve-watcher' overstated it.
- (event) Surface reconcile 2026-10-24 (Story 37.12 review 5, read-only PASS): .github/workflows/kedro-viz-publish.yml and src/shared/packages/pyforge-atlas/tests/unit/test_vizro_static_boards.py (comments only: the snapshot history is appended to by each run whose rollup has rows, not every run; the workflow counts seven raw entries that are no last-good store, as the test does). Corrects the Story 37.12 reconcile event above: the test file gains six Story 37.12 tests, not seven (13 -> 19), and the deferred-work ledger's sub-row count 209 was stale (213 at the base), so '209 -> 216' is a correction plus three new rows (DW-atlas-37-12-1, -2, -3).
- (decision) 2026-10-24 Story 37.13 (CAP-69, CAP-64; a fix: no new CAP, no flag beyond Story 37.5's pyforge.atlas.board_projections; closes DW-atlas-37-12-3): the cve-watcher never presents an earlier run's diff as current. SnapshotHistoryDataset.save leaves the AD-13 marker <store>.staleness.json (StalenessMarker: stale, reason, marked_at, last_good_exists) when it appends no snapshot (an empty frame, or a store it cannot read), and removes it after a save that appends one; a frame without the key columns still raises. With the flag on, the cve-watcher's AD-17 stamp comes from the snapshot store and the diff file together: either one unavailable wins (no store is a data gap, not an empty table stamped now), else the older of the latest snapshot_at (kind snapshot-at) and the diff file's mtime, and a store carrying the marker (or a marker that cannot be read) adds 'stale: <reason>' to the stamp line. Kept the earlier diff rather than writing it empty: an empty table reads as 'nothing moved', which is as false as an old diff stamped now. diff_vulnerability_snapshots, its rows and columns, the catalog and the node count do not change; with the flag off, the board reads primary/vuln_history and its file's stamp as before. Why: Story 37.12 caches the snapshot history on Pages but never stores/vdb; a failed vdb build leaves the rollup empty, the store unchanged, and the diff equal to the run before, under a stamp that was the diff file's fresh mtime.
- (event) Surface reconcile 2026-10-24 (Story 37.13, CAP-69 / CAP-64; branch claude/nervous-knuth-8b42e2; reviews 1-2, every finding fixed or recorded as a residual risk): src/shared/packages/pyforge-atlas/src/pyforge/atlas/datasets/snapshot_history.py (the AD-13 marker <store>.staleness.json on a save that appends no snapshot, removed after a save that writes rows; read_staleness), src/shared/packages/pyforge-atlas/src/pyforge/atlas/provenance.py (resolve_for_snapshot_history: the latest snapshot_at as kind snapshot-at, with the marker's reason), src/shared/packages/pyforge-atlas/src/pyforge/atlas/dashboard/app.py (_resolve_cve_watcher_provenance for the flag-on cve-watcher; _provenance_line adds a reason after an available stamp; the cve-watcher note) and src/shared/packages/pyforge-atlas/src/pyforge/atlas/dashboard/data.py (VULN_ROLLUP_SNAPSHOTS_PARQUET); src/shared/packages/pyforge-atlas/tests/unit/test_cve_watcher_staleness.py (added), src/shared/packages/pyforge-atlas/tests/unit/datasets/test_snapshot_history.py (the marker), src/shared/packages/pyforge-atlas/tests/unit/test_board_projections.py (the flag-on cve-watcher is stamped from its snapshot) and src/shared/packages/pyforge-atlas/tests/unit/test_board_wiring.py (the diff's and the store's paths are their catalog filepaths); _bmad-output/projects/pyforge-atlas/planning-artifacts/deferred-work-ledger.md closes DW-atlas-37-12-3. The diff node, the catalog and every other board's stamp are unchanged.
- (decision) 2026-10-24 Story 27.6 (CAP-8, the read surface; a fix: no new CAP, no FR, no flag; closes DW-atlas-retro-2026-10-15-1): test_declared_controls_render_against_real_rows waits for the AG Grid to mount inside #distribution-breakdown--grid (.ag-root-wrapper attached, 15 s, the budget its filter and chart waits use), then reads the linux-64 row. Why: Vizro builds every AgGrid as an empty placeholder and fills it from the page's on_page_load callback; the static facet filter (Story 27.5) and the chart's container render from the layout, so the three 15 s waits pass before that callback runs and the grid assertion alone carried the callback, the browser applying its output and AG Grid's lazily loaded script, under Playwright's 5 s default. Measured: the callback answers within 130 ms at every Chromium CPU-throttling rate (1x-24x), so the time is the browser's; 32 copies of the real test on 16 xdist workers beside 48 busy loops failed 4 of 24 with the preflight's message. Rejected: a retry or rerun (hides a real missing row, AD-11); a longer wait on the row text alone (a grid that never mounts and a grid without the row then read the same). Epic 27 owns the test (Stories 27.3, 27.5) and reopens until 27.6 lands.
- (event) Correction 2026-10-24 to the Story 27.6 decision entry above (the memlog is append-only): the mount wait comes first and carries the page's whole render with 60 s, the budget this suite gives a loaded host (the dashboard fixture's 60 s server bind, the WASM smoke's 60 s ready wait); the filter, dropdown, chart and row checks follow unchanged. Why: with the 15 s mount wait placed after the chart, the load harness (32 copies, 16 xdist workers, 48 busy loops) still failed 3 of 24 copies in one of three batches, on the dropdown's own 15 s wait. Timed under that load, the grid mounted up to 23 s after the page opened (up to 16 s after the chart) and the dropdown, drawn just before the grid, up to 20 s; at 32 busy loops the mount took up to 21 s and the dropdown up to 17.5 s. So per-step budgets do not fit the page; one budget on the element drawn last does. A grid that never mounts still fails on the mount wait (now after 60 s), and a mounted grid without the row fails on the row within 5 s, showing the grid's text.
- (event) Correction 2026-10-24 (Story 27.6 review 1, FAIL, every finding fixed or answered) to the two Story 27.6 entries above: (1) the lazily loaded script is dash-ag-grid's grid chunk (async-community.js: ag-grid-react and the Dash wrapper), not AG Grid's core, which ships in the eager dash_ag_grid.min.js; (2) the networkidle wait is dropped, so the mount wait follows goto directly (networkidle settled before the callback output was applied and carried render time on its own 30 s default; goto keeps its 30 s load default, measured at most 4.0 s under load); (3) the element order was re-measured in the page with a MutationObserver under 48 busy loops (25 copies): the filter and chart were visible 3.9-16.9 s and the dropdown 0.4-16.3 s before the grid mounted, the mount came 6.3-20.8 s after navigation with the linux-64 row within 1 ms, and the on_page_load request took 0.4-1.9 s, so the browser, not the Vizro server, holds most of the time (CPU throttling alone said nothing about the server under host load); (4) a wait on the row text alone was not rejected because it reads a never-mounted grid like a grid without the row (to_contain_text prints the actual text either way) but because the whole render budget would sit on the row, so a real missing row would take 60 s to report. The row check keeps its default 5 s.
